**⛔ READ `C26` FIRST**, two sections of it in particular: `#### Sub-step 4, the benefit` is the
evidence for everything below, and `#### The constant moved` is what shipped on 2026-08-19. **This is
not a duplicate of C26.** C26 is a page-wide **threshold** on this mechanism
(`textPageInkOutsideThreshold`, 0.08 -> 0.045) and it was the right call on the arithmetic it had.
This entry is the mechanism the threshold stands in for, and the reason a threshold cannot be the
final answer: measured, it **overpays on pages that lose nothing and still leaves pages that do**.

**The one-sentence statement.** On the layered (MRC) route the 1-bit stencil is the *intersection* of
an adaptive binarisation of the whole page with the geometry of the words Vision recognised — so ink
the recogniser missed is in neither the stencil nor the text layer, and survives only in the
background, which on a page read as all text is stored at **1/8** of the page's resolution. C26's
sub-step 4 read thirteen such pages at 1:1 and **8 were losing content outright**: thirteen values out
of a Pearson correlation matrix on `Xin Qu et al_2018` p20 (✅ **re-measured independently 2026-08-20
and it holds — they are the matrix's last column, stencil ink `0.0000`**), seven lines of prose on
`_1973_Committee Against Racism_` p4, three lines on `Broadhead - 1994` p6. Content present in the
source, absent from the text layer, illegible in the image, and **nothing reported it until
2026-08-20** — invariant 1; the run report names those pages now, and `#### The report, SHIPPED` is
what it says and what it deliberately does not do.
**Measured 2026-08-20, it happens on pages the shipped bar still shrinks too**: of the eight pages
nearest the bar from below, **4 of 8 lose content** — two lines of prose on `Broadhead - 1994` p3, two
on `Jones et al_2010` p5, table figures on `Scott_TK` p3, a handwritten signature on `Atkinson_1939`
p3 — and sorted by `inkOutsideText` those four are the 1st, 3rd, 6th and 8th, so **the signal the bar
reads does not order the loss**. ⛔ **And measured the same day over 24 more pages, it happens a third
of the way to zero**: `Jones et al_2010` p7 loses the paper's **estimating equation** at `inkOut`
**0.0137** and its p2 loses a word at **0.0008**, so the population at risk is not "the pages near the
bar" — 27 of the 73 print `0.0000`, one page at 0.0008 is already losing type, and sub-step 4
found one of those `0.0000` pages losing a pencilled annotation. ⛔ **And
measured the same day over the twenty highest-`inkOut` pages left, the loss is a MIDDLE band**: 8 of
20 lose content, their `inkOut` runs [0.0051, 0.0165], and of the twelve that lose nothing **four sit
above that band and six below it** — so the signal does not merely fail to separate the two sets, it
is not monotone in the direction `pageIsAllText()` assumes, and the four pages with the most ink
outside the recognised words are the first four any bar protects and lose nothing at all.
⛔ **And the last 21 of the 73, rendered the same day, close the population and refute the bar from the
bar cannot reach it**: 2 of the 21 lose content — the words `their education,` and `but` on

`_1939_Former students` p2 at an `inkOut` that **prints `0.0000`**, where no usable bar exists. So the

2026-08-21 by `#### A shape term, MEASURED`: 12 type and 5 hand-made with `Atkinson_1939` p3 in both**,
so the two buckets no longer partition the 16 (12 + 5 − 1 = 16); the 16 does not move.

#### The mechanism, read off the shipped code (read, not executed) — 2026-08-19

`Flattener.mrcLayers` builds the stencil in three lines, `Sources/Flattener.swift:2634-2638`:

```swift
var mask = sauvolaMask(grey, width: w, height: h, window: …)  // every ink pixel on the page
…
let region = textRegionMask(boxes, width: w, height: h)       // the recognised word boxes, padded
for i in 0..<(w * h) where !region[i] { mask[i] = false }     // the stencil is the INTERSECTION
```

Four facts about that, each checked in the source rather than assumed:

1. **`sauvolaMask` sees everything and `region` throws most of it away.** The binarisation is adaptive
   and page-wide; the confinement is `textRegionMask`, which fills only the recognised boxes plus a pad
   of `mrcBoxPadding` = **0.25** of each box's *height* — `padX = padY * h / w` in normalised
   coordinates, which is the same pad in **pixels** on both axes rather than a wider one horizontally.
   Nothing else contributes to the stencil.
2. **`boxes` is exactly the text layer's geometry.** `Sources/Model.swift:2144` passes
   `byPage[index + 1]?.map({ $0.boundingBox })` — the same recognition observations
   `SearchableWriter` draws its runs from. So "outside the stencil" and "not searchable" are the same
   set by construction, which is what makes the image the *only* remaining copy of those marks.
3. **What is left outside is stored at the background factor.** `mrcLayers` takes
   `backgroundDownsample` from `settings.photoDetail.downsample` — **1 / 2 / 3** for Maximum /
   Balanced / Smallest — and then, on a page `pageIsAllText()` accepts, raises it to
   `max(caller, textPageBackgroundDownsample = 8)` with the foreground at `max(caller, 16)`. Maximum
   short-circuits the whole rule (`keepEveryPixel`), so the 8x case is Balanced and Smallest, i.e. the
   shipped default and below.
4. **The 1-bit route does not have this defect.** `bilevelImage` thresholds the whole page with no box
   confinement, and `Model.swift`'s re-layering loop skips bilevel pages outright ("A bilevel page is
   already cheaper than MRC could be"). Unrecognised ink survives there at 1-bit fidelity — subject to
   R56's own thresholding harm, which is a different entry. **This is a layered-route defect only.**

`textRegionMask` has exactly **one** production call site and `inkOutsideText` exactly one (by grep
over `Sources/`), so the whole of this behaviour is those two functions and the three lines above.

**The sibling sweep (CONTRIBUTING 4b) found no second site making the same inference.** Grepped over
`Sources/`: nothing else reasons from "not inside a word box" to "not text". `Flattener.swift:52` is
ink *coverage*, a different signal, and `Model.swift:2145`'s `!boxes.isEmpty` guard is the one place
the shape is benign — a page where Vision recognised **nothing** is skipped by the re-layering loop
entirely and keeps its full-resolution JPEG. ⚠️ Note what that implies about severity: the defect is
worst where recognition mostly *works*, because a handful of missed lines on an otherwise clean page
is exactly what produces a low `inkOutsideText` and therefore the 8x shrink. Total failure is safe;
near-success is not.

**The same sweep over the documents found the premise stated in three more places, two corrected here
and one deliberately left.** Corrected in this commit: `R50`'s entry ("ink that is not inside any
recognised word is not text") and the matching sentence in `HANDOFF.md` §R50, both of which now carry
a ⛔ pointer here — R50 stays `FIXED`, because what is wrong is its justification and not its fix.
**Left alone: `CHANGELOG.md`'s `1.12.0` note**, which says the app "can now tell, because layering
happens *after* recognition and ink that falls outside every recognised word is not text". That is a
released note and a record of what was claimed at the time; the `## Unreleased` section carries the
correction instead, where a reader looking at the current state will find it. Stated rather than
silently skipped, per CONTRIBUTING 4b.

#### The premise this entry refutes WAS written in the code, as a fact

`inkOutsideText`'s own doc comment (`Sources/Flattener.swift:1411`), as it read up to and including
`783b1a5` — the commit that moved the constant:

> This runs *after* recognition, where the word boxes exist, so it can ask a structural question
> instead of a statistical one: **ink that is not inside any recognised word is, by construction, not
> text.**

That sentence is the justification for both the signal and the confinement, and C26's sub-step 4
measured it false on **7 of 13 pages**: the ink outside the recognised words was running prose or
table data on `Xin Qu et al_2018`, `_1973_Committee Against Racism_`, `Broadhead - 1994` (three
pages), `Atkinson_1939` and `Glazer_2002`. It is false for an ordinary reason — Vision misses lines on
faint, skewed and tightly-set scans — and the code has no term that can tell "the recogniser missed
this" from "this is not text". The comment already carried a ⛔ correction later in the same block
saying words Vision did not box are destroyed at 1/8; the two paragraphs contradicted each other, and
the premise is corrected in the same commit that opens this entry. **No behaviour changed with it** —
the comment is the only thing that moved.

#### And the same bar overpays, which is the other half

From C26 sub-step 4, unchanged here and cited rather than re-derived: `RIESMAN_1942` p10 is rank 1 of
the 16 by cost (**+702,280 B, 6.47x**) and the only ink outside its stencil is a pale scanner-edge
strip carrying show-through, barely legible in the source. With `Riesman - 1954` p18 (+258,183 B,
show-through only) that is **32.4% of the band's +2,965,653 B spent on two pages a reader cannot tell
apart.** A page-wide ink fraction cannot separate those from `Xin Qu` p20 because both are "ink
outside the words"; the difference is *what the marks are*, which is the question this entry is about.
⛔ **A third such page was measured 2026-08-20**: `1976 - Regis McKenna Papers` p4, **+350,632 B at
7.89x** — the steepest ratio in this campaign — whose out-of-stencil ink is platen strips and a staple
shadow and nothing else. ⚠️ **But the sub-step that found it also weakened this section's general
claim, and that is recorded there rather than here**: over its twenty pages a bar set to protect every
loser puts only **37.6%** of its cost on pages that lose nothing, against 57.4% (sub-step 1) and 56.6%
(sub-step 2). How much a bar overpays is a property of the pages sampled, not a constant.

#### The eight near-misses, RENDERED — 4 of 8 lose content, and `inkOutsideText` does not order them — MEASURED 2026-08-20

**This is the first sub-step of this entry and it answers the first of the questions below on the
eight pages that question named.** All eight rendered, all four documents, exit 0 on every
invocation, `INKDUMP` reporting 7 files written in full per page with none missing, and the tool's
own per-page check saying the **stencil is byte-identical at both bars on all 8** — so the whole
difference between the two images compared below is the background factor and nothing else. `inkOut`
and `layered` reproduce `INKBAR-2026-08-19.tsv` **digit for digit on all eight rows** — which pins
determinism across invocations, not independence: the sweep drives this same binary, and those two
columns do not depend on `INKBAR`. (The first draft called it "a second code path"; the audit of this
diff was right that it is one code path run twice.)

⛔ **FIRST, A CORRECTION TO THIS ENTRY'S OWN RECIPE, AND IT WOULD HAVE READ AS "LOSES NOTHING".**
The instrument line below said `INKBAR=0.08 INKDUMP=<dir>`, copied from C26 sub-step 4 where the
comparison was the old bar against the new one. **For this population that recipe compares a page
with itself.** These eight sit *under* the shipped 0.045, so at 0.08 they are `all-text` on both
sides. Measured on `Broadhead - 1994` p3: `barVerdict` **`all-text`**, `barDelta` **`same`**,
background **284 px shipped vs 284 px at the bar**, and the two dumped JPEGs are **byte-identical**
(sha256 `10abe386c157b468…` for both). A reader following the recipe would have diffed two copies of
one file and concluded the page loses nothing — on the page that turns out to lose two lines of
prose. **The bar must be BELOW the page's own `inkOut`**, which is what makes production's verdict
the shrunk one and the override's the un-shrunk one; `INKBAR=0.02` covers all eight (the lowest
`inkOut` here is 0.0353). Backgrounds then come out **1139 px against 284 px** (Broadhead, 2278 px
render), 510/127 (Scott, 1020), 967/241 (Atkinson, 1935), 620/155 (Jones, 1240) — the caller's 2x
against the all-text 8x, as fact 3 of the mechanism says.

**The verdicts, each settled by a 1:1 crop and each cross-checked against the stencil's own ink in
that rect.** Order is by `inkOut`, descending, i.e. the order the table above lists them in:

| `inkOut` | page | what is outside the stencil | as shipped | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.0450 | `Broadhead - 1994` p3 | **2 lines of body text** at the head of p155 — *"alizing a cultural order at that moment passing from life and of fab- / ricating, in the literary realm, a mentally possessible version of a"* | illegible blur | **LOSES WORDS** |
| 0.0441 | `Broadhead - 1994` p10 | page edges, gutter, the scanner's thumbs | — | nothing a reader sees |
| 0.0412 | `Scott_TK_The Scientific Selection of Salesmen` p3 | a scattered subset of the numerals in **four of the five columns** of the salesmen table (column C keeps only a sub-glyph fragment), the **column heads B / C / E**, the **Roman-numeral row labels** (IX., X., XI., XV.), the table rules, several **bold display lines** (*"by means of carefully classified data"*, *"tons during the last few years"*, *"roll of"*, *"Follow-"*, *"1918 Ap-"*, *"power sell."*) and the **"Digitized by Google / Original from UNIVERSITY OF MICHIGAN"** footer | blobs and smears; not one figure readable | **LOSES TABLE DATA** |
| 0.0410 | `Broadhead - 1994` p12 | page edges, gutter, thumbs (an endnotes page) | — | nothing a reader sees |
| 0.0405 | `Broadhead - 1994` p5 | page edges, gutter, thumbs | — | nothing a reader sees |
| 0.0388 | `Atkinson_1939` p3 | the **handwritten signature** on a 1939 letter of resignation, and the typed dash rule under it | a smeared wavy band; no stroke followable | **LOSES A HAND-DRAWN MARK** |
| 0.0370 | `Broadhead - 1994` p7 | page edges, gutter, thumbs | — | nothing a reader sees |
| 0.0353 | `Jones et al_2010` p5 | **2 lines of body text** — *"whereas potential dilution is known ex-ante. Another rea- / son for using potential dilution is that other Finnish"* — plus Table 2's head rule, the `11111111111` **Pattern cell** (11 digits, one per year 1992-2002) and two bold Frequency values | lines illegible; rule → grey band; cell → smudge | **LOSES WORDS** |

**Read as counts: 4 of 8 lose something, 3 of those 4 lose words or table data and the 4th a
hand-drawn mark, 4 lose nothing a reader would see.** Sub-step 4's 13 pages on the other side of the
bar split 7 words-or-table-data to 1 hand-drawn mark — the same two buckets in the same order, and
**not** the same ratio (3:1 here against 7:1 there); the first draft of this line called it "the same
3:1 shape", which is a reproduction claim that does not reproduce, and the numbers audit of this diff
caught it.

⛔ **AND THE SHARPEST RESULT IS THAT NO VALUE OF THE CONSTANT SEPARATES THESE EIGHT PAGES.** Sorted by
`inkOut` the eight read **L, n, L, n, n, L, n, L** — losses at ranks 1, 3, 6 and 8, the *lowest* of the
eight (`Jones` p5, 0.0353) losing two lines of prose and **two of the four highest** losing nothing.
The two sets **interleave**: `pageIsAllText()` reads a page as all text when
`inkOutsideText(…) < bar` (`Sources/Flattener.swift:2695-2697`, strict `<`; the shrink additionally
needs `!keepEveryPixel`), so a page keeps its resolution when `inkOut` is at or above the bar, and
protecting all four losers needs a bar at or below **0.03525** — the sweep prints four places, so
`Jones` p5's true value is in [0.03525, 0.03535) — which protects all four non-losers too, since the
lowest of them is at least 0.03695. There is no `textPageInkOutsideThreshold` that gets these eight
pages right: not 0.045, not 0.035, not any value.

⛔ **And the second term does not rescue the argument — it separates the eight PERFECTLY IN THE WRONG
DIRECTION, which is sharper than a tie.** Sorted by `extent` the eight read **n, n, n, n, L, L, L, L**:
the four highest (0.03460 / 0.03260 / 0.03159 / 0.02959) are exactly the four pages that lose nothing
and the four lowest (0.02457 / 0.00053 / 0.00000 / 0.00000) exactly the four that lose content. So no
*pair* of constants works either — any `paleDrawingThreshold` low enough to leave the non-losers shrunk
is above `Jones` p5 and `Scott_TK` p3, which sit at 0.00000 and are unreachable by that term at any
positive value. ⚠️ This paragraph first said `paleDrawing` "rescues none of them at any bar", which is

`Atkinson_1939` p3. Corrected by the adversarial review of this diff, which also found the ordering. That is this entry's thesis measured rather than argued, and it is a stronger statement
than the byte arithmetic ever made: the term has to be about *what the marks are*, which is question 3

that the other three documents "have no such window"; the numbers audit of this diff refuted it from

**Method, and it is C26 sub-step 4's with one addition that made it cheap.** The addition: at the bar,
ink outside the stencil appears as **true glyphs** while ink inside it appears only as a pale
`fillHoles` residue, so the un-normalised at-bar background *is* the locator — one 700 px look per
page says where to crop, and on four of the eight says there is nothing to crop. Every verdict then
rests on a three-row 1:1 crop (source / at bar / as shipped), and **three of the four** losses are
additionally cross-checked against the **stencil's own ink fraction** in that rect, which is the part
that does not depend on anyone's eye:

| rect | stencil ink | adjacent control, in the stencil |
|---|---|---|
| `Atkinson_1939` p3, the signature | **0.0%** | 7.6% (the typed line above) |
| `Broadhead - 1994` p3, the two lost lines | **0.13%** | 11.4% (the next two lines) |
| `Jones et al_2010` p5, the two lost lines | **3.6%** | 15.4% (the third line, which survives) |

for its table rect is owed and is cheap. So is one for each of the four negatives, where the check
would be the other direction: high stencil ink everywhere there is ink.

readings were overturned by the crops *and* independently by the stencil's ink fraction. Do not read
a normalised whole-page image as evidence in this entry; it is a locator and a bad one.

⚠️ **One test this sub-step did NOT run, and the audit of the diff both proposed it and had to run it

Composited at 1:1 on the riskiest of the three (`Jones` p5, whose lost rect still holds 3.6% stencil
ink), the two lost lines are **illegible** while the in-stencil lines above and below them are sharp in

here was itself the instrument failing**: the image it produced holds three grey levels and no JPEG at
all — it was the stencil over a flat grey field, with the blurred background, the one thing that might
have restored legibility, absent. It looked like a composite and was not one. §3 again, and the reason

**The byte price of the eight, which is the other half of any fix and is now measured on this
population rather than the band.** 430,679 B shipped → 1,754,076 B un-shrunk: **+1,323,397 B,
4.07x, 165,425 B/page**, all of it tone layers. Per page, `4.35x / 4.21x / 3.15x / 3.97x / 4.20x /
4.44x / 4.25x / 3.30x` in the table's order. That is in line with the band's measured 4.54x and
185,353 B/page (C26 sub-step 3b) rather than cheaper — and **more than half of it buys nothing**,
since the four pages that lose nothing account for 759,536 B of the 1,323,397 (**57.4%**). Which is this
entry's thesis restated in the residue: a page-wide bar cannot spend that money where the words are.

**What remains of question 1: 65 of the 73.** Eight rendered, four losing. Seven of the eight came out
of the 31 in the six documents sub-step 4 already read (`Scott_TK` p3 is in none of them), so **24 of
that 31 remain** and are still the cheapest place to continue. ⛔ **Those 24 were rendered on

#### The other 24 in the six read documents, RENDERED — the loss reaches a THIRD of the bar, and quantity does not order it either — MEASURED 2026-08-20

**The population.** The 24 pages of the 73 that sit in the six documents C26 sub-step 4 rendered and
that sub-step 1 did not reach: `Xin Qu et al_2018` p3/p5/p8/p10/p13/p23/p25/p28,
`Riesman - 1954` p6/p10/p12/p14/p20/p24, `Jones et al_2010` p2/p3/p7/p9,
`_1973_Committee Against Racism_` p1/p2/p3, `Broadhead - 1994` p2/p4, `Atkinson_1939` p1. Six
invocations, `rc=0` on every one, `INKDUMP` **7 files per page with none missing on all 24**, and
`inkOut`, `layered` and `extent` reproduce `INKBAR-2026-08-19.tsv` **digit for digit on all 24 rows**
(checked by a script over the committed file, not by eye).

⛔ **FIRST, A BOUND ON THE OVERRIDE SEAM ITSELF, AND IT IS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN A BUG: 11 of these
24 CANNOT BE PRICED THROUGH IT, AND NEITHER CAN 27 OF THE 73.** `pageIsAllText()` compares
`inkOutsideText(…) < bar` with a strict `<`, so un-shrinking a page needs a bar **at or below** its own
`inkOut`; `Tools/score-text-route.swift` refuses an `INKBAR` outside `(0, 1)`, for the good reason its
own header gives. A page whose `inkOut` is **0** therefore has no legal bar, and 11 of these 24 read
`barDelta` `same` at `INKBAR=0.00001` — `Atkinson_1939` p1, `_1973_Committee Against Racism_` p1/p2/p3,
`Jones` p3/p9, `Riesman - 1954` p6/p10/p14, `Xin Qu` p3/p5. The sweep prints four places and **27 of
the 73 print `0.0000`**, so this is not a corner: it is a third of the population. ⚠️ Stated exactly:
**no legal bar exists only for a page whose `inkOut` is exactly 0**; what is *measured* is that 11 of
these 24 do not flip at 1e-5 and 27 of the 73 print `0.0000`. A page at, say, 3.3e-6 is positive,
priceable at 1e-9, and indistinguishable from 0 at both four places and 1e-5, and it is the same
false-negative shape sub-step 1 corrected (two byte-identical backgrounds read as "loses nothing")
arriving from the other side. ⚠️ Printing `0.0000` is *not* the test — `Riesman - 1954` p12 prints
`0.0000` and **did** flip, so its true value is in [0.00001, 0.00005). The test is `barDelta`.
⚠️ And the summary line cannot show which tiny bar was used: it formats the bar with `%.4f`, so
`INKBAR=0.00001` prints as `INKBAR 0.0000 against the shipped 0.0450`.

**So this sub-step used a second instrument, and it needs no override at all.** From the dump's own
`-source.png` (the grey render) and `-stencil.png`, the marks that survive **only** in the background
are `ink AND NOT stencil`, where ink is the same page-wide Otsu the guard uses:

```sh
magick "$s-source.png" -auto-threshold OTSU -negate ink.png
magick "$s-stencil.png" -morphology Erode Disk:3 notsten.png   # dilate the stencil's INK by 3 px
magick ink.png notsten.png -compose Multiply -composite out.png # 0/255 AND; Multiply is commutative
```

⚠️ **Both halves of that recipe are there because the first version was wrong, measured.** (a) Without
the erosion the map is dominated by an artifact: `inkOutsideText` thresholds with a page-wide Otsu
while the stencil comes from an adaptive Sauvola mask, so every stencilled glyph contributes a
one-or-two-pixel rim of "ink not in the stencil" — 34,158 px on `Atkinson_1939` p1, **9.1% of its
interior ink on a page whose `inkOut` is 0.0000**. Eroding by 3 px takes that page to **15 px**.
⚠️ **The rim is not a constant multiple, and this sentence claimed "3-5x of `inkOut`" until the review
of this diff measured all twelve positive-`inkOut` pages**: the r=0 fraction over `inkOut` runs
**0.97x to 19.4x** — ~1.0x on the eight `Jones`/`Xin Qu` pages (where the rim is negligible:
`Jones` p7 goes 2,248 → 2,208 px, `Xin Qu` p10 151 → 151), 3.3x/3.6x on the two `Broadhead` pages and
8.0x/19.4x on `Riesman` p20/p24. `Atkinson_1939` p1 is the worst case, not the typical one. (b) The first script used
`-compose Minus`, which computes `dst - src` and so measured the *stencil* minus the ink; it was caught
by a dilation sweep in which the counts **rose** with r, and every number it produced is void.

✅ **The locator is validated against a positive and a negative control with verdicts already settled
by eye.** Re-rendered `Broadhead - 1994` p3 and p10 at `INKBAR=0.02` (`inkOut` 0.0450 / 0.0441 and
`layeredAtBar` 244,731 / 248,701 B, reproducing sub-step 1's 4.35x / 4.21x): on **p3** the map holds
exactly the two lines sub-step 1 read by eye — *"alizing a cultural order at that moment passing from
life and of fab- / ricating, in the literary realm, a mentally possessible version of a"* — and on
**p10** it holds page edges, the gutter and the scanner's thumbs and no type at all. It does not
merely agree with the earlier verdicts; it **names the words**.

⛔ **AND THE CONTROL PAIR KILLS A QUANTITY TERM AS SHARPLY AS SUB-STEP 1 KILLED A BAR.** p3, which
loses two lines, holds **15,431 px** of out-of-stencil interior ink (4.48% of its interior ink); p10,
which loses nothing, holds **15,727 px** (4.48%) — the non-loser is *higher* — and both are flat across
`r` = 2 / 3 / 4 (15,495 / 15,431 / 15,377 and 15,750 / 15,727 / 15,711), because what dominates both is
the gutter shadow, a solid region no erosion touches. So "how much ink is outside the stencil" orders
the loss no better than "what fraction of the page's ink is outside the words". Question 3's term has
to be about *what the marks are*, and this is the second measured refusal of a scalar.

**The verdicts.** Each mark located by the map, each verdict settled by a 1:1 three-row crop

**The stencil-ink cross-check, which is the part that does not depend on anyone's eye:**

✅ **AND THE PUBLISHED PAGE WAS COMPOSITED FOR THE RISKIEST VERDICT, WHICH IS THE TEST SUB-STEP 1'S
AUDIT ASKED FOR — AND IT CORRECTED THIS SECTION'S OWN FIRST DRAFT.** The page a reader sees is the
stencil over the background, not the background alone, so a mark read as lost off the background could
be rescued by stencil ink on top of it. Composited at 1:1 on `Jones` p7
(`magick bg-shipped.jpg -resize WxH! stencil.png -compose Multiply -composite`, **88 unique grey
levels**, which is the check that it is a real composite and not last session's flat-field failure):
the prose above and beside the equation is **sharp**, the equation number `(2)` is sharp, and — ⛔
against what the first draft of the table above claimed — the `ε_it ~ iid(0, σ²); i = 1,2,…,N;
t = 2,3,…,T` line **also survives sharp**, which its 2.87% stencil ink already said. What is illegible
in the composite is the **two displayed lines of the equation** (0.00% stencil ink) and the
`(p-value 0.12).` parenthetical lower down, and the running-head rule is simply absent. So the verdict
holds and is narrower than first written: two equation lines and a p-value, not the whole display.
Caught by running the test rather than by reasoning about it.

| rect | stencil ink | adjacent control, in the stencil |
|---|---|---|
| `Jones` p7, the two equation lines — `470x56+100+742` | **0.00%** | 15.59% (the prose line immediately above, `470x24+100+714`) |
| `Jones` p7, the `iid` line — `400x26+140+808` | 2.87% — **survives** | — |
| `Jones` p2, "value." — `46x7+640+831` | **0.00%** | 11.94% (the line above, `200x16+560+806`) |

**Read as counts: 2 of 24 lose content, 1 more degrades a hand-drawn mark, 5 blur a graphic rule, and
16 lose nothing a reader would see.** Sub-step 1's eight split 4 losing to 4 not; these 24 split 2 to
22 — and they interleave here too. Sorted by `inkOut` descending the 24 are ranked 1 `Broadhead` p4
(nothing), 2 `Broadhead` p2 (nothing), **3 `Jones` p7 (the equation)**, 4 `Riesman - 1954` p20 (the pen
line), 5-11 the `Xin Qu` pages and `Riesman` p24, **12 `Jones` p2 (the word)**, 13-24 the twelve pages
printing 0.0000 (nothing). So the two highest of the 24 lose nothing while ranks 3 and 12 lose type:
the same shape as the eight, one order of magnitude lower down.

⚠️ Two honest limits on that table. The first rect measured for `Jones` p2 read **12.26%** and was
simply in the wrong place — 30 px off, landing on stencilled type; the map's own bbox gave the rect
that reads 0.00%. And a 0.00% stencil in a rect has two possible causes, `region` excluding it or
Sauvola never calling it ink; the first is C28's mechanism and the second would be a different defect,
but **the harm is identical either way** — absent from the 1-bit layer, absent from the text layer by
fact 2, stored at 1/8.

⛔ **THE RESULT IS THAT THE LOSS REACHES A THIRD OF THE WAY TO ZERO.** Before today the lowest measured
loser was `Jones et al_2010` p5 at 0.0353. `Jones` p7 loses a paper's **estimating equation** at
**0.0137** — 3.3x below the shipped bar, 2.6x below the lowest of the eight near-misses — and `Jones`
p2 loses a word at **0.0008**. Displayed mathematics is a failure class this campaign had not seen:
Vision boxes prose and misses set equations, so the marks most reliably outside the stencil are exactly
the ones a reader cannot reconstruct. **17 of the 73 sit at or above 0.0137 and 27 sit at exactly
0.0000**, so "the pages near the bar" was never the population.

⛔ **AND THIS SUB-STEP COMPLETES THE PER-DOCUMENT PICTURE FOR ALL SIX, WHICH PRODUCES THE STRONGEST
RESULT IN THE CAMPAIGN: ONE SCAN DOES NOT ORDER ITS OWN PAGES.** The window-relevant set of a document
is the pages whose `verdict` at 0.08 is `all-text` — the ones the first term can move — and with these
24 every such page in all six documents has a verdict. ⚠️ **A first draft of this paragraph said "two
documents, and they are disjoint"; the adversarial review of this diff refuted it from the committed
sweep, and what it found is sharper than what was claimed.**

- ⛔ **`Riesman - 1954` has NO window.** Its **p18 at 0.0676 loses nothing** (C26 sub-step 4:
  show-through, fingers and the page edge) while its **p16 at 0.0565 loses a hand-drawn margin bracket

⚠️ **One structural note this sub-step is the first to quantify: `inkOutsideText` drops the outer

of `Blacks in the City` over any usable bar), but it means the signal is blind to a sixteenth of the
sheet it decides the resolution of. Measured out-of-stencil ink in that band on these 24 runs to
**283,499 px** (`Broadhead` p2), **308,622** (p4) and **176,290** (`Riesman - 1954` p24, which includes
characters from the facing sheet). ⚠️ **A first draft said "nothing belonging to the page itself was found there"; the review of this diff
found a counter-example and the claim is withdrawn.** Of the six bands over 50,000 px

founding-mode loss (`Riesman - 1954` p16's hand-drawn bracket) lived in an outer margin too.

#### The twenty highest-`inkOut` of the remaining 41, RENDERED — 8 of 20 lose content, and the losers are a MIDDLE band with non-losers on both sides — MEASURED 2026-08-20

**The population, and why these twenty.** The section above says to pick by `inkOut` descending, so:
the **twelve** highest-`inkOut` pages of the 41 (`1976 - Regis McKenna Papers` p4 down to
`Merriam_1913` p2, 0.0287 to 0.0063), **plus the completion of the two documents where completing
them was nearly free** — `Ford_1941_Speech_` (all 6 of its sub-bar pages, 5 of them extra) and
`Stanford_1891_Jane Stanford opening day speech` (all 4, 3 extra) — because a whole document is the
only way to ask whether a bar orders that document's own pages, which is where sub-steps 1 and 2 got
their sharpest results. Twenty pages, **eleven documents, none of which had been looked at** (the
twelve highest span eleven too — `_1958_Executive Pay` contributes two; a first draft of this
sentence said ten and was contradicted by the word "eleven" three words later). Eleven
invocations, `rc=0` on every one, `INKDUMP` **7 files per page with none missing on all 20** (140
files), and `inkOut`, `layered` and `extent` reproduce `INKBAR-2026-08-19.tsv` **digit for digit on
all 20 rows** — checked by a script over the committed file, not by eye.

✅ **And all twenty are reachable through the override seam**, unlike sub-step 2's 11-of-24 floor:
every one printed a changed `barVerdict` at its document's bar, so every row below has both a byte
price and a render pair. The bars used were strictly below the lowest printed `inkOut` in each
invocation — 0.02 / 0.01 / 0.005 / 0.0005 / 0.00004 — which is the rule the tool's header states.

⛔ **THE HEADLINE IS THAT THE BAR'S ORDERING IS WRONG AT THE TOP OF THE RANGE: THE FOUR PAGES WITH
THE MOST INK OUTSIDE THE RECOGNISED WORDS LOSE NOTHING.** Sorted by `inkOut` descending, the twenty
read

```
n n n n L L n n L L L L L L n n n n n n
```

The eight losers span `inkOut` **[0.0051, 0.0165]**. Of the twelve non-losers, **four sit above that
whole span**, **six below it**, and **two inside**. `pageIsAllText()` is one-sided — a page is
protected exactly when its `inkOut` is *at or above* the bar — so those four are the first four any
bar protects as it comes down, and each of them loses only a staple-and-debris shadow, magazine column
rules, a book's gutter shadow, or a photograph's frame.

sub-step 1's finding reproduced in a fresh population, not a new one. What survives as new is the
top of that string: in sub-step 1 the highest-`inkOut` page of the eight was a loser, and here the
four highest of the 41 are not.

⚠️ **TWO INSTRUMENT CORRECTIONS, AND THE FIRST ONE INVALIDATED THE LOCATOR'S FIRST RUN.**

1. **The map's published RECIPE has to crop to the interior — both images — because `inkOutsideText`
   does.** The guard walks x ∈ [w/16, w−w/16) and y ∈ [h/16, h−h/16) and divides outside-ink by
   *interior* ink, so it never sees a photographed surround or a platen edge. Counted over the whole
   page the locator's fraction reads **0.4479** on `Ford_1941` p1 against an `inkOut` of 0.0051 and
   **0.8199** on `Regis McKenna` p4 against 0.0287 — and the largest *ratio* is neither of those but
   **256x**, `Stanford_1891` p4's 0.0256 against 0.0001, with `Ford_1941` p4 at 143x (⚠️ a first
   draft led with "88x", which is Ford p1's ratio and only the largest absolute *fraction*). Cropped,
   the twenty read **0.56x to 16.0x**. ⚠️ **Two corrections to how this is stated.** (a) The crop must
   be applied to the ink image as well as the map — the fraction is outside/ink, and cropping only the
   numerator gives 0.0048 rather than 0.0087 on `Ford_1941` p1, an error of 1.8x in the direction that
   hides the problem; the first version of the recipe added to `score-text-route`'s header cropped
   only the map. (b) **Sub-step 2's own numbers were ALREADY interior** ("9.1% of its interior ink",
   "4.48% of its interior ink"), so its 0.97x-19.4x band is *not* void and is still the reference used
   above — it was only the shell recipe it published that omitted the crop, and that is what this
   sub-step followed. ⚠️ And what the outer band holds is **not established here**: `Ford_1941` p1's
   band carries 224,288 px of out-of-stencil ink and none of the twenty outer bands was inspected mark
   by mark, while sub-step 2 explicitly *withdrew* the claim that such a band holds nothing belonging
   to the page (`Riesman - 1954` p6's held a typographic mark). "Photographed surround and platen
   edge" is what these pages look like, not a measurement.
   ⚠️ **Six of the twenty read BELOW 1.0** — `_1967_Ampex` p1 0.56x, `Merriam_1913` p2 0.57x,
   `_1958_Executive Pay` p5 0.72x and p3 0.77x, `UN-OCred` p7 0.90x, `Gitlin_2000` p1 0.98x — and
   **the cause is not established.** A first draft attributed it to the `Disk:3` erosion eating marks
   within 3 px of stencilled ink; that is reasoned, and sub-step 2's own r=0-to-r=3 numbers move about
   2% on such pages, which cannot produce 0.56x. Two other differences are candidates and neither was
   isolated: ImageMagick's whole-page OTSU against `otsuThreshold(of:)`, and the fact that the dumped
   stencil is the Sauvola mask **∧ `region`** while the guard tests `region` alone — which should push
   the map *higher*, not lower. An r=0 interior run over these twenty would settle it and was not done.
   Either way the map is a locator and not a measure.
2. **The fraction interleaves as badly as the count did — the third measured refusal of a scalar, and
   the first on the fraction form.** Sorted by the locator's interior fraction the twenty read
   `L n n n L n n n L L L L L n n L n n n n`: `Merriam_1913` p2 loses words at 0.0036 while
   `Stanford_1891` p1 and p2 lose nothing at 0.0050 and 0.0041, and `Regis McKenna` p4 is second of
   twenty at 0.0339 and loses nothing. Sub-step 2 killed the pixel *count*; this kills its
   normalised form on the same page set that killed the bar.

#### The last 21 of the 73, RENDERED — question 1 is CLOSED: 16 of the 73 lose content, and the page holding the MOST ink outside the recognised words loses nothing — MEASURED 2026-08-20

**This is the fourth and final sub-step, and it finishes the population.** 8 + 24 + 20 + 21 = 73, so
every page still shrunk 8x/16x at the shipped bar has now been rendered at both factors and read.
**Of the 73, 16 lose content** — 11 lose type and 5 lose a hand-made mark — by addition over the four
sub-steps (4 + 2 + 8 + 2), not by re-measuring. With C26 sub-step 4's 13 pages that is a campaign
total of **86 pages rendered, 24 losing content, 18 of them type and 6 a hand-made mark**.

**The one loss, and the evidence for it.** `Williams_1958_DEMOCRACY OR MERITOCRACY` p1 is a 1958
Manchester Guardian page. The map's 1,493 interior pixels are **not** scattered: 998 of them are 21
components inside one rect, `249x18+711+1490`, and 214 more are 4 components inside
`45x17+936+1508`. The map holds **1,544** white pixels page-wide, 1,493 of them interior and 1,521 in
components of ≥8 px — so those two rects on two adjacent lines hold **1,212 of the 1,521**, and the
remaining 309 are twelve specks of ≤75 px scattered elsewhere. Those two rects are the words **`their education,`** on
the line *"…their education, their occupation and…"* and the word **`but`** on the line below,
*"…their power, but according to their…"* — the comma belongs to *power*, and a first draft wrote
`but,` and mis-transcribed the first line's tail; corrected by the review of the diff against the crop. At 1:1, source / stencil / map / composite-at-bar /
composite-as-shipped: the source is sharp, the stencil is blank where those words are, the bar's
composite reads them, and **the shipped composite reads `▬▬▬ ▬▬▬▬▬▬ their occu` and `their power, ▬▬
accor`** — an unresolved smear with no letterforms. The composite is stencil-over-background (160
unique grey levels in the rect, which is the check that it is a real composite and not a flat field), so this is
what a reader gets, not the background alone.

**And the cross-check that does not depend on anyone's eye — a better one than this entry has been
using.** Rather than the stencil's ink fraction in the lost rect against an adjacent survivor's, take
the **stencil's ink over the *source's* ink in the same rect**, which normalises for how much ink is
there at all:

| rect | what | source ink | stencil ink | stencil/source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `249x18+711+1490` | `their education,` — lost | 45.07% | **9.62%** | **0.21** |
| `200x18+970+1490` | `their occupa` — survivor, same line | 47.36% | 51.64% | 1.09 |
| `45x17+936+1508` | `but` — lost | 39.22% | **6.80%** | **0.17** |
| `230x17+700+1508` | `their power,` — survivor, same line | 27.54% | 29.18% | 1.06 |

A survivor's stencil holds essentially all of its ink; the two lost rects' stencils hold a fifth of
theirs. ⚠️ **But it is a WITHIN-PAGE contrast, and the margin for any corpus bar on it is narrow and
rests on two pages.** `Herbert Marks` p12's densest map bands read stencil/source **0.40-0.47**
and its two unflagged control bands **0.52-0.57** (bands `533x20+526+907`, `722x19+655+974`,
`702x37+605+450`, `709x35+568+722`, `709x37+628+990`, `528x36+772+1083`; controls `600x30+300+600` and
`600x30+300+1300`), on a page that loses nothing. ⚠️ **This read "seven densest bands" and "thirteen
rects" while listing six and twelve; the review of this diff counted them.** The enumerable figure is
what stands — **six** bands, **twelve** rects — and whether a seventh band was measured and dropped in
transcription is not recoverable from what was recorded, so treat the twelve as the sample.
⛔ So over these twelve rects the ratio does **not**
interleave — sorted it reads `L L n n n n n n n n n n` and a bar at 0.3 orders all twelve — which is
what a first draft of this paragraph claimed and the review of the diff refuted. What can be said is
that such a bar would sit in a **0.21-to-0.40 gap established on two pages**, untested; and that the
copies of this note in `Tools/score-text-route.swift` and `QUEUE.md` state the limit without the
interleaving claim. This entry's three *measured refusals* remain `inkOutsideText`, the map's pixel
count and the map's fraction — this ratio is a candidate, not a fourth refusal.
⛔ **Both ranges in that sentence REPRODUCE at all four bounds and the SEPARATION BETWEEN THEM DOES
NOT SURVIVE being measured per line — see the sub-section below, 2026-09-21.**

##### The two control bands, READ — and the separation is the BANDS' framing, 2026-09-21

⛔ **THE CONTROLS ARE NOT THE PROBLEM AND THE BAND RECTS ARE. Read per line, the 0.40-0.47 / 0.52-0.57
separation that makes the candidate bar's `0.21-to-0.40` gap a gap falls from **0.0352 to 0.0025**
page-wide and, on the recipe this entry actually prescribes, from **0.0414 to −0.0074** — it
INVERTS.** The step asked whether `600x30+300+600` and
`600x30+300+1300` — the only two rects in this entry chosen around no mark at all — read **0.52-0.57**
as an artefact of their own emptiness. They do not, and the reason they do not is the finding.

✅ **First, both published ranges REPRODUCE, through a different binary 31 days on, at all four
bounds** — bands **0.3957-0.4742** and controls **0.5156-0.5671**, i.e. `0.40-0.47` and `0.52-0.57`
exactly. ⛔ **But only through the header's own recipe, which crops FIRST and takes Otsu PER CROP, and
that had to be established rather than assumed.** A page-wide Otsu (213/255) on the same eight rects
gives **0.3942-0.4804** and **0.5156-0.5533**, moving **six of the eight** by up to 0.020 and two
(`722x19`, `600x30+300+1300`) not at all. **The four-of-four reproduction is the whole argument and
needs nothing else** — ⚠️ a second one corroborates it with its own limit named: on this page the
stencil is a **measured strict subset of the page-wide ink map (0 of 57,308 px outside)**, which
would cap the ratio at 1, while this entry's own `Williams_1958` p1 survivors read **1.09 and 1.06**
— ⛔ **but the subset property is measured on p12 ONLY and is not structural** (Sauvola is adaptive
and a global Otsu need not contain it), so that half is an inference about Williams p1 and not a
reading of it. Either way a re-measurement must say which recipe it used.

✅ **Read at 4x, both controls hold ONE LINE of the page's own typescript** — `in the matter of` and
`of the Public Uti`, on the right 52% and 55% of their 600 px — and **all eight rects hold the same
material**, 1940s typewriter type on one sheet: no rule, no half-tone, no paper-only rect. So the
worry's literal form is refused by the reading. ⚠️ Five of the eight were read at 4x, the other three
at 2.5x in one montage.

⛔ **Blank paper inside a rect is NOT ratio-neutral on the prescribed recipe, and the obvious test of
that CANNOT FAIL on the other one.** Page-wide, tightening each control to its own inked box leaves
numerator and denominator as the *same integers* — 1,314/727 and 1,346/694 — so 0.5533 and 0.5156 come
back unchanged **by entailment, not by measurement**, the stencil being a subset of the ink map and
the discarded margin having no ink by construction. Per crop the same tightening moves
**0.5671 → 0.5747** and **0.5156 → 0.5226**, because blank paper shifts the crop's own Otsu: +0.008
and +0.007, about **18% of the 0.0414 per-crop band-to-control gap**, and in the direction that makes
a loose control read LOW. The controls are loose — `600x30+300+600` discards **47.7%** of its columns
(inked 265…578) and 23% of its rows, `600x30+300+1300` **44.7%** and 17% — so the effect is real,
small, and does not rescue the published gap.

⛔ **WHAT DOES DESTROY IT IS THAT NONE OF THE SIX BANDS IS A LINE.** Four straddle two lines, carrying
a sheared glyph-top of the line above (`702x37`, `709x35`, `709x37`, `528x36`), and the other two
(`533x20`, `722x19`) are single-line windows that still clip their line's ascenders and descenders —
`533x20+526+907` sits inside a line inking y 902…932. Both controls, by contrast, contain their line
whole with blank rows above and below. Re-read per line from each rect's own row profile, all six
against both controls:

| reading | b533 | b722 | b702 | b709a | b709b | **b528** | ctrl1 | ctrl2 | gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| as published (rects), per crop | 0.4056 | 0.4594 | 0.4051 | 0.4742 | 0.3957 | 0.4412 | 0.5671 | 0.5156 | **0.0414** |
| per line, per crop | 0.4274 | 0.4696 | 0.4293 | 0.4829 | 0.4115 | **0.5230** | 0.5671 | 0.5156 | **−0.0074** |
| per line, page-wide | 0.4209 | 0.4696 | 0.4397 | 0.4829 | 0.4236 | **0.5131** | 0.5533 | 0.5156 | **0.0025** |

⛔ **So on the prescribed recipe the highest band's own line reads 0.5230 against the lower control's
0.5156 and the two populations cross; page-wide they are 0.0025 apart, which is a fourteenth of the
published 0.035.** ⛔ **And the row that does it — `528x36+772+1083`, whose line inks y 1104…1131 — is
one of the two bands a first draft of this sub-section never split**, that draft having split only
`709x37` and `709x35` and concluded from them that the separation *widened* to 0.055. It does not; the
adversarial review of that diff found the unsplit row and the whole finding inverted.
⚠️ **The asymmetry is what the published comparison is measuring, and it is exact**: restoring the line
raises the ratio on **6 of 6** bands (per crop **+0.009 to +0.082**, page-wide +0.003 to +0.057) and on
**0 of 2** controls, which move by **0.0000** — the controls' rows are trimmed in that table at the
same full rect width as the bands', so the two arms get the same treatment, and the controls already
contained their line. ⚠️ Trimming the controls' *width* as well is a different operation and does move
them, per crop, to 0.5747 and 0.5226; the table does not do it, because the bands cannot be
width-trimmed — all six have an ink bounding box equal to their own rect.
⚠️ Every line span is read off that rect's own row profile between troughs, verified for all six
(`902…932`, `969…998`, `462…494`, `736…764`, `1003…1032`, `1104…1131`).

⚠️ **The threshold is worth less than a draft of this claimed, in both directions.** The ratio moves
**~0.0067 per grey level** of the page-wide Otsu, measured per rect over 209/213/217 as
**0.0055-0.0079** — not the 0.011 a draft printed, which is 1.6x the mean and 1.4x the maximum and
came of reading a two-level step as one. ⛔ **And the movement is COMMON MODE, so the gap is nearly
threshold-invariant**: bandMax/ctrlMin read 0.5104/0.5482, 0.4804/0.5156, 0.4576/0.4912 and the gap
0.0378/0.0352/0.0336 — **0.00053 per level, so ~67 levels would be needed to close it**. Dividing the
gap by the within-rect slope, which gave a draft's *"the whole gap is 3.2 grey levels wide"*, is a
units error that draws the opposite conclusion from the same sweep. ⚠️ The band/control **partition**
holds at all three thresholds; the full eight-rect **ordering** does not — `722x19` and `528x36` swap
between 209 and 213, which printing only bandMax/ctrlMin hides.

⛔ **The step's own box named the wrong rect for the upper end**: `709x37+628+990` reads **0.3957**
per crop, the **lowest** of the six, where the maximum is `709x35+568+722` at **0.4742**. Both were
read anyway.
✅ **A cross-artefact control that could have failed, and a stale-news trap avoided**: the run prints
`verdict picture`, `lineN` **2**, `layered` **92,052 B**, which reproduces
`SHAPETERM-BYTES-2026-08-21.tsv`'s p12 row and this entry's own table at
`| 0.0041 | Herbert Marks papers p12 | 23,452 -> 92,052 (3.93x) |` **digit for digit across 31 days
and two binaries**. ⛔ A draft reported it as the discovery that *"p12 no longer routes as the
paragraph above describes"*; it was published a month ago, p12 being one of the 16 pages the shape
term's wiring already refuses. ⚠️ `INKBAR-2026-08-19.tsv`'s `layered` is the 0.08 arm and is only
comparable because `inkOut` 0.0041 sits below both bars.

⚠️ **Predictions, and the one that was wrong.** Four were written down before any crop was rendered,
off the source-ink column alone. (1) *not blank paper* — held, 1,314 and 1,346 source-ink px against
the bands' 2,027-3,231. (2) *type that does not fill the band* — held exactly. (3) *not a rule* —
held. (4) *the comparison stays confounded* — **held in substance and wrong in its mechanism**: the
draft predicted a DENSITY confound (controls 7.30%/7.48% against bands 12.08-20.11%) and then
retracted it on the tightening test, which is the test that cannot fail; per line the confound is real
and is **line-clipping**, not density. ⛔ **The retraction was the error, not the prediction.**
⚠️ One page, eight rects, one document, one render; the entry's other page (`Williams_1958` p1, a
different document) was not re-read, and **no bar is proposed** — this ratio is still a candidate and
not this entry's fourth refusal. Nothing in `Sources/` moved and no artefact was written. Reproduce
with `INKDUMP=<dir> score-text-route "testdocs/document/Herbert Marks papers.pdf" 12`, then
`magick <rect-crop> -auto-threshold OTSU -negate` **per crop** over `-source.png`, against the plain
black-pixel count of `-stencil.png` in the same rect.

⛔ **THE MAP'S FRACTION IS REFUSED AGAIN AND THIS TIME FROM THE TOP — the same third refusal
reproduced in a fresh population, not a fourth one.** `Herbert Marks` p12
holds the **highest** out-of-stencil fraction of all 21 — 0.04775, **37x** the fraction of the one
page that loses content and **11.65x its own `inkOut`** — and it loses nothing: it is a pale
typescript, so the page-wide Otsu the map uses catches a rim that the adaptive Sauvola stencil does
not, on every glyph on the sheet. Four bands were read at 1:1 (`AGE Corp. - Employment (HSM)`,
`…politan Edison Company`, `…kinsville Water Company - corresponde`, `…andum of Pending Legal
Matters assigned`) and all are legible as shipped, softer than at the bar. Its 209 components of
≥8 px have a **largest of 49 px**: there is no type-shaped mark on the page, only 2,575 px of rim in
209 pieces. ⚠️ **So `frac`/`inkOut` over the campaign's cropped maps now spans 0.00x to at least 35x** —
`Guilford` p1 reads an empty map against an `inkOut` of 0.0031, `_1939` p7 reads the top of the range —
widening sub-step 3's 0.56x-16.0x in both directions. ⚠️ **The table prints that top ratio as 53.94x
and it should not be read to four figures**: `inkOut` is printed to 4 dp, so a denominator shown as
0.0001 is anywhere in [0.00005, 0.00015) and the ratio is anywhere in (35.9x, 107.8x]. The review of
this diff sized that; the claim is ">35x". The map locates; it does not measure.

**The thirteen settled by count, and the count is robust to the erosion radius.** `Krippner` ×10,
`Guilford` ×2 and `Riesman_1973` p5 read 0-34 interior pixels at r=3. Because these are small renders
(1014x1614 and 920x1428) where a 3 px disk is proportionally large, the four densest were re-run at
**r=1**, the most permissive legal radius: `Krippner` p34 goes 34 -> 867 px, p10 20 -> 251,
`Guilford` p1 0 -> 133 — and the **largest interior component in any of them is 8-10 px** (a 5x3,
a 6x2, a 7x2). The four quiet `_1939` pages behave the same way at r=1 — p2 **955 px in 12** components, largest
`12x47` (309 px); p3 **1,372 in 33**, largest `19x53` (904 px); p14 195 in 13, largest 15x8; p16 120 in
13, largest 3x6 — the two largest being the photographed sheet's own edge. ⚠️ **A first draft quoted
646 and 468 in 11 and 32, which is each of those figures with its largest component silently dropped**,
in the paragraph whose point is that nothing type-shaped survives; caught by the review of the diff
re-running the command. Nothing type-shaped survives it, so the *Otsu* verdicts do not rest on the
radius — but see the next paragraph for what Otsu cannot see at any radius.

⛔ **AND THE MAP'S GLOBAL OTSU IS BLIND TO A PALE MARK ON A SHADOWED SHEET, WHICH IS HOW THIS
SUB-STEP'S SECOND LOSS WAS NEARLY MISSED.** `inkOutsideText` and this map both threshold the whole page
at one level, and `_1939_Former students` p2 is a photographed handwritten letter whose right-hand
margin runs 0.36-0.74 grey: the pencil script there is *paler than the page's own Otsu*, so the map
reports **44 px** in `140x205+1660+2125` and **1 px** in `180x120+1640+1580` where an **adaptive**
threshold (`-lat 25x25-8%`, then `AND NOT dilate(stencil)`) reports **2,069** and **1,371**, against a
stencil holding 46 and 43 px. At 1:1 the source shows continuous cursive strokes and the shipped
composite shows disconnected specks with no followable stroke — the same standard by which
`Atkinson_1939` p3's signature and `Ford_1941` p1's pencilled annotation were counted as losses.
⛔ **And this is the entry's sharpest page, because p2's `inkOut` prints `0.0000` and its `barDelta` is
`same`: production's own first term is blind to exactly the ink that is lost, so no *usable* value of
`textPageInkOutsideThreshold` reaches this page.** ⚠️ Stated exactly, since the seam bounds rather than
measures it: `barDelta same` at `INKBAR=0.00001` puts `inkOutsideText` in **[0, 1e-5)**. If it is
exactly 0 then the guard's strict `<` means only a bar of **0** refuses the shrink, and a bar of 0
refuses it on all 16,987 pages; if it is positive then the bar that reaches it is under 1e-5, some
**4,500x** below the shipped 0.045 and three orders of magnitude below the lowest this campaign has
priced. Neither reading leaves a bar anyone would ship, which is the claim — not that the numerator is
provably zero. The two failures are opposite and both belong
to a global threshold — it invents a rim on a pale typescript (`Herbert Marks` p12) and misses real
pale ink on a shadowed sheet (`_1939` p2). ⚠️ The adaptive map was run over all 21 pages by the review
of this diff and broke none of the other nineteen negatives: `Guilford` p1 reads 17 px, p5 0,
`Riesman_1973` p5 1, the ten `Krippner` pages 138-315 px with no component ≥150, and `Herbert Marks`
p12 10,328 px still with no component ≥150 — so those verdicts are now stronger than the Otsu map alone
made them.

⛔ **AND `Disk:0` IS NOT "NO EROSION" — measured, and it would have inverted a dilation sweep.**
`Guilford` p1 reads **0 px at `-morphology Erode Disk:0`, 133 px at `Disk:1`, 15 at `Disk:2`, 0 at
`Disk:3`**. A monotone erosion cannot rise from 0 to 133 and fall again, and
`-define morphology:showKernel=1` says why: **`Disk:0` prints a 9x9+4+4 kernel — radius 4** — against
3x3, 5x5 and 7x7 for `Disk:1/2/3`. So it is ImageMagick's default radius, *larger* than `Disk:3`; the
series is monotone once r=0 is read as r=4, and the r=0 column of such a sweep is mislabelled rather
than merely void. Start at r=1. (The kernel print is the review of this diff's measurement; a first
draft said only "some larger default".) This is the same shape as the `-compose Minus` error sub-step 2 recorded: a dilation sweep
whose counts move the wrong way is the instrument, not the page.

⚠️ **ONE MORE INSTRUMENT NOTE, FROM A SLIP MADE AND CAUGHT IN THIS SUB-STEP.** ImageMagick's
`-connected-components` reports coordinates in the frame of the image it is given, so a run over the
**interior-cropped** map needs `+mx+my` added to reach page coordinates and a run over the **whole**
map needs nothing added. Both were used here, the offset was added to both, and the result was a
confident reading of a region 200 px away from any flagged ink — where the map is genuinely blank and
the page genuinely loses nothing, which is a **false negative that looked like a careful negative**.
What caught it was a direct white-pixel count in the rect: `400x60+690+1478` holds 1,212 of the page's
1,544 map pixels and `40x40+1050+1700` holds **0**. So before believing a component rect, count the
pixels inside it — the arithmetic between two coordinate frames is exactly the kind of step that
produces a plausible wrong answer.

**What this sub-step does not change.** The mechanism, the premise, and questions 2-4 below are
untouched: no production code moved, and `textRegionMask` still keeps only what was recognised. What
it changes is that question 1 is answered over its whole population instead of a sample, and that the
two remaining arguments for a page-wide bar are both now measured false — it protects the wrong end
of its range first (sub-step 3), and below it the pages that need nothing outnumber the ones that do
about 37 to 2, while a page that loses a pencilled annotation sits at `inkOut` `0.0000` where the bar
has nothing at all to say (this one).

5. ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-20 — the run report now names every page it stored at an eighth.** The finding
   stands as written: `inkOutsideText` was read exactly once, in the **layering** decision inside
   `mrcLayers` — not the route, which `isPicture` settles before recognition and which never reads it
